Doc lane: `src/tests/crdt/arch-v2/i3-capture-window.test.ts` (K15 pin: previews and commit written across gaps longer than `captureTimeout` stay one stack item when `lastChange` is refreshed before each write; control: 4 items, one undo leaves `noteにほん`). **cdp** (`ime-baseline.cdp.spec.ts`, 20 tests with the smoke file): new F-I16 (b) and F-I17 (10 s pause) written red on the reference (`note한` after undo; the idle cancel deleted the preview); **flipped green**: F-I16 (a), (b), (d), F-I17, F-I6, and the four F-S12 session contracts (mid-word, bold, peer same block, peer other block: one `compositionstart`, one `compositionend`, no `Selection` mutator call while the session lives, the IME's node intact, the preview rendered once); still red: F-I15 (I4: the empty filler is kept during the session but the branch switch at the release drops the IME's node). The F-I16 (d) row's `readDomText === ''` was a harness error (an empty text renders its `\u200B` filler); it now strips the filler, as I2's helper does. On the real IME path the reference's own caret write after each preview ended Chromium's composition (a `compositionstart` per update, the commit arriving as a plain `insertText`), which is why F-I16 (a)/(d) could not pass before. **Design** (`session/composition.svelte.ts`, O34, L7): `start()` at `compositionstart` (the handler first reads the DOM selection) records the start target (the selection value), captures the marks once (`insertionMarks` → `marksForInsertion`), pins the host (`Text._acquireCompositionPin(from, to)`: the frozen render + the base without the replaced target); a live session receiving a new `compositionstart` is abandoned first (D-7). The first write opens the session: `dispatcher.cut('composition')` (policy row `composition: 'both'`), the start target replaced by `replaceSelection…TargetSync` inside `dispatcher.scope` (hooks, veto — a veto marks the session refused: it writes nothing and the tail inverts what the browser showed), the region start minted bound left. `update(value, attempt)` = mechanical tracked write in one transaction, no hooks: delete the live items between the region's anchors per stream, insert `value` with the captured marks at the region start, re-mint the end anchor bound left to the preview's last atom (so a peer's insert right after the preview stays outside — `composition-remote-lock` "remote insert after" — and one inside is absorbed); the capture group is held open before each write (`um.lastChange = Date.now()` when the stack top is the session's item, K15); the model caret is `select()`ed only. One ending `#end(value)`: `commit(value)` (`insertFromComposition`, a text insertion while live, `compositionend` — three signals, one implementation) = one user command: `dispatcher.dispatch('insertText', {value, start, end: start, marks})` whose body deletes the preview per stream and inserts — hooks and `onAfterOperation` see one collapsed insertion of the final value (a hook's replacement applies, e.g. auto-pair); a refused commit erases the preview; `cancel()` (empty final value) erases inside the group; `abandon()` (focus loss, a non-composing keydown, a pointerdown, a new `compositionstart`) commits `host._imeBuffer()` — the host's DOM text between the pinned base's two sides — else the model's preview. Then `cut('composition', 'after')`, the pin released, the catch-up display (`stabilizeCompositionSelection`: the IME post-commit jump rule, kept for V5), `observer.flushNow()` for the records deferred while live, and the `ended` listeners. Tail: `phase` `tail` until the next non-composition occurrence (`occur`, a non-composition `input`, any non-phantom keydown) or `TAIL_MS` = 500 compared at use; while it lasts a trailing `compositionend` is swallowed, a late composition `input` admits a model-owned `restore` drift attempt with the committed caret (repaired by `onInput`'s drift repair), the observer inverts a divergent tail host instead of adopting it, and the first Enter (Backspace on WebKit) with a collapsed caret is the phantom key (`swallows`, read by DST as `postCompositionGuardSwallows`). A composition `input` no `beforeinput` announced (Android) takes what the host shows as the preview. Render: while the browser shows the preview itself (its `beforeinput` was not prevented) the pinned render and the empty filler stay frozen, so our renders never rewrite the IME's node; a prevented (script) update renders base + preview as before. `onSelectionChange` ignores DOM selection changes while a session is live (composition noise; the end displays). Composition `beforeinput`s arm no drift expectation. **Deleted** (L35, L30's windows): `compositionState` (numbers `startOffset/value/regionLength`, anchors re-minted on every sync, `restoreSelectionAfterCommit`), `compositionStartReplacementState`, `hasHandledCompositionInput`, `_compositionHostText`, `compositionText`, `resolveCompositionRegion`, `Text._syncCompositionRegion`/`_reanchorCompositionRegion`/`_resolveCompositionOffset`/`toCompositionDomOffset`, the `deleteAt` region branch, the pin's `caretAnchor`, the lazy pin acquisition in `refreshFromProject`/`_setItems`, `_bind`'s `textId` follow; `onCompositionEnd`'s two commit paths, `insertCompositionText`/`insertFromComposition`/`commitCompositionFromInsertText`/`finishCompositionFromBeforeInput`, the `input`-channel state-only end (4 commits → 1); the observer's `cancelIdleCompositionPreview` + 750 ms timer and its restore branch, the dangling-blur 50 ms reset (2 cancels → 1 + abandonment); `driftAfterComposition` (the 50 ms post-commit window); `shouldIgnoreCompositionKeyDown` + `compositionEndedAt`; `resetCompositionIfNeeded`, `NON_COMPOSITION_INSERT_TYPES`, `markCompositionInputHandledIfNeeded`, `rememberInterruptedCompositionSelectionIfNeeded`; `refreshEditorDom`'s 16 ms polling while composing (now `composition.ended`). **Census** (vendor excluded): setTimeout 36 → 32 (idle cancel, dangling blur, refresh poll ×2); composition flags on the runtime root 6 → 0 (`isComposing` is a getter of the session phase); rAF 8, `await tick()` 18, `flushMirror(` 15, `stopCapturing()` 1, `instanceof PreventionError` 1, `ignoreNextSelectionChange` writes 15, MutationObserver 4, `flushSync(` 0 — unchanged; type-body 934 (unchanged); vendor Δ +27. **API**: `Edytor.composition` (new; the session), `Edytor.isComposing` is read-only; removed `compositionState`, `compositionStartReplacementState`, `hasHandledCompositionInput`, `compositionText`, `resolveCompositionRegion`, `_compositionHostText`, `shouldIgnoreCompositionKeyDown`, `Text.toCompositionDomOffset`; `postCompositionGuardSwallows` is a getter; `insertionMarks` exported from `beforeInputCommands`; the slash menu opens on an insertion that starts with `/` (an IME commits `/h` as one insertion). **For V4's gate** (the single facts): `edytor.composition.host` (the `Text` whose element a live session owns; `null` unless live, reactive), `edytor.composition.owns(node)`, `edytor.composition.live` / `.phase` (reactive `live | tail | gone`), and `edytor.composition.ended(fn)` (runs once after the live session's ending — its writes, pin release and catch-up display — or at once when none is live). **Deviations**: (1) the previews no longer write the DOM selection and the host render freezes while the browser shows the preview — this is V4's gate/§1.3 IME-node territory, but without it Chromium restarts the composition after each preview and F-I16 (a)/(d) cannot hold on the real path; it flipped the F-S12 cdp contracts, which V4's merge must not re-mark `test.fail`. (2) `composition.spec` "reconciles composition DOM mutation when beforeinput is missing" asserted the session ends without `compositionend` (the idle cancel); it now asserts the session stays live until its `compositionend` (D-7: time never ends a session); two other specs stop reading the removed `compositionState`. (3) the hotkeys fixture "keeps Emacs bindings inert while composing" now sends a composing key (`isComposing: true`): a non-composing key abandons the session (D-7). (4) the F-I16 (d) cdp harness strips the empty filler. **Design iterations**: one — the first cut minted the region end bound right at the first write (a peer insert right after the preview was absorbed: remote-lock "remote insert after" red) and read `interrupted` literally (a delete inside the region lost the caret: cancellation specs red); the end is now re-minted bound to the preview's last atom and an interrupting command's caret is kept only outside the region. **Contract answers** (coordinator, binding): (1) after abandonment a non-composing key acts normally — accepted (D-7); (2) `TAIL_MS` = 500 ms compared at use — accepted; (3) an IME-committed `(` reaching hooks as one `insertText` and being auto-paired — accepted (plugins see one insert). The questions as asked: (1) abandonment on a non-composing keydown lets the key act afterwards (the reference swallowed it and reset 50 ms later): DST's `·`/`é` Firefox sequences now type the next key instead of counting a designed swallow — confirm. (2) The tail's observer/`input` ownership is bounded by `TAIL_MS` = 500 ms compared at use (the phantom-key window reused); the plan leaves the value open. (3) An IME-committed insertion is shown to hooks as one `insertText` of the final value (auto-pair on a committed `(` now pairs it) — confirm this is the intended D33 reading. |

*line/document extension moves the focus*: Shift+Home then Shift+End keeps the anchor at the caret, Shift+PageUp is reversed then Shift+PageDown shrinks (**red**: the reference anchored at the document-order start); *vertical* (O44): Shift+ArrowDown past a collapsed toggle's body and Shift+ArrowUp into it from below (**red**: landed in the hidden child), Shift+ArrowDown into a list lands in its first item (**red**: the container's phantom slot), the goal column over a shorter line (pin). Browser `tests/editor-dom/arch-v2-v6-navigation.spec.ts` (6 × 3 engines, `?scenario=v6&dst=`): RTL word keys move visually (Alt/Ctrl+ArrowLeft = forward; **red**: moved logically), RTL ArrowLeft at a block end crosses forward and back (pin), RTL Shift+ArrowLeft at a range focus at the block end (**red** on all three: Chromium/WebKit no move, Firefox one grapheme), the collapsed toggle (**red**), a selected block-end atom extends across the edge (**red**), a reversed focus after a block-start atom steps over it then over the block edge (**red**: native stopped at the block start). **Owner**: `session/navigation.ts`. `displayable(edytor, from, dir)` = the next block in `facade.order()` whose own content the Surface displays (`selection.displayable(id)`, V3's `#displayable` made public); `step(stop, dir, 'char' | 'word')` = a grapheme/word boundary inside the text, else the near side of the text beyond the adjacent atom, else the near edge of the next displayable block; `edge(block, dir)`; `move(edytor, unit, key, extend)` is the one apply step: endpoints from the projection's compatibility state (an atom value is its surrounding range, extended from the side opposite the direction), the direction of `left`/`right` from `selection.rtl(focus text)` (a new one-line Surface fact next to `displayable`), a plain key collapses a non-collapsed selection onto its edge (a text range with a grapheme arrow is left native), the focus moves by the unit, `select(anchor, focus)` writes once: a caret, the atom when the range covers exactly one, else the document-ordered range with its direction (the reference's state-then-DOM double writes and its pre-collapse `clearInlineBlockSelection` go). Native iff the selection is text-bound and the step stays inside the focus's text (char unit only); node-bound (`hasNativeNodeSelection`, K10) is always the model's, keeping the forward canonicalization off another block's first stop and the collapse when nothing is next. **Bindings as rows**: 16 `[unit, direction, platform?]` rows (arrows, Home/End, PageUp/Down, Mod+Up/Down, Alt- (Apple) and Mod- (else) word keys, Emacs ctrl+a/e/b/f), each non-Emacs row also bound with Shift; one `bind` closure. **Vertical** (K1, O44): `extendVertically(edytor, dir)` moves the document-order edge nearest the motion one logical line (texts split at `\n`, an atom one column) with the goal column keyed on the selection value it produced; blocks are crossed through the same `displayable` walk, sealed at island boundaries, voids passed over. Plain Up/Down stay native. **Deleted** (L53): `hotkeys/navigation.ts` — the five walkers (`getPrevious/NextWordPosition`, `moveAcrossHorizontalBoundary` + `getAdjacentEditableText`, `getHorizontalExtendDestination`, `moveToCurrentBlockBoundary`/`moveToDocumentBoundary`), `moveByWord`, `moveNodeSelectionHorizontally`, `getInlineBlockBetweenBoundaryPositions`, the duplicated focus/anchor extraction and `compareTextPositions`, 26 binding closures; `bindings.ts`' `motion`/`step` helpers and four Emacs closures; `EdytorSelection.extendSelectionVertically` + `#verticalExtendGoal` (string signature); `selection.utils.ts`' `VisualLinePiece`, `visualLinesOfBlock`, `destinationOnVisualLine`, `locateVisualLine`, `islandRootOfBlock`, `getVerticalLineDestination` (a sixth walker, over `closestPrevious/NextBlock` with no displayable filter). **Census** (ref → V6): unchanged — setTimeout 20, rAF 5, `await tick()` 12, `flushMirror(` 15, `stopCapturing()` 1, `instanceof PreventionError` 1, `ignoreNextSelectionChange` writes 0, MutationObserver 4, `flushSync(` 0, type-body 967, vendor Δ +27, named browser rules 5 (no new timer, flag or retry). **Behavior changes** (all follow from one apply step): Shift+Home/End/PageUp/PageDown and Mod+Shift+Up/Down move the focus from the anchor (the reference anchored at the document-order start, so Shift+Home yielded a forward range and Shift+Home→Shift+End selected the whole block); a word extension that reaches exactly one atom from a non-collapsed range extends the range (the reference replaced it by the atom alone); word keys are visual under RTL; a Shift+Arrow from a text range whose focus is at an atom or a block edge, and from an atom selection, is the model's; a plain arrow off an atom writes one selection (was two `onSelectionChange`s). **Deviations**: (1) *Vertical folded in*: V6's row names only L53, but the vertical destination walked `closestPrevious/NextBlock` itself (entering hidden toggle bodies and list container slots); moving it onto the stream's displayable walk is the O44 owner the plan names (§3.2) — its rows were added as a second tests-first commit (b2a177d); `surface/geometry.ts` is not created (the lines were never layout: `\n`-split logical lines). (2) *One row corrected after its tests-first commit*: the vertical toggle row's second step first asserted focus semantics (Shift+Up after Shift+Down shrinking back to the caret); K1's documented rule moves the start edge going up, so the step became Shift+ArrowUp from the block below (still red on the reference: it landed in `two`). (3) Not in V6: plain ArrowUp/Down, the block-selection keys, `selectNextVoidBlockFromCaret`. **Contract questions → answered (coordinator: both yes) and fixed in 074488f** ("V6 fix: focus-moving vertical extension; atom selection keeps its anchor side"): (a) Shift+ArrowUp/Down now moves the **focus** and keeps the anchor, like every horizontal key and native Blink/WebKit (Shift+Down then Shift+Up returns to the original caret); K1's "document-order edge nearest the motion" rule is removed (`extendVertically` takes anchor/focus from the value's direction). (b) The atom `SelectionValue` carries `from: 'before' | 'after'` (`AtomSide`): a Shift key or a DOM range collapsing onto exactly one atom sets it from the direction, a click or programmatic `selectInlineBlock`/`atomSelection` defaults to `'before'`; `sameValue` compares it; navigation treats the atom as a range anchored on that side, so Shift+Right then Shift+Left shrinks back to the caret. Presence: **unchanged** — `serialize(value)` still publishes `{atom, block}` without the side (the smaller change; a peer's caret rendering does not need it). Rows (red on 3e7e241, green after): dom — Shift+Down then Shift+Up → the original caret; Shift+Up from a caret extends upward, Shift+Down shrinks back; Shift+Up on a forward range moves its end; caret before an atom → Shift+Right (atom, `from: 'before'`) → Shift+Left → the caret, and the mirror from after; a programmatic atom selection is anchored before; the vertical toggle row restored to the round trip (its V6 correction to K1 withdrawn, citing the answer), the block-start-atom row now selects the atom from after before extending across the edge. Browser (three engines): the Shift+Down/Up round trip then Shift+Up from the caret, the two atom round trips. Retained surface, green before and after (7): `connect: false` defers the dial and `status` runs connecting → connected → disconnected; a dropped socket redials within `maxBackoffTime`; liveness closes and redials a socket silent past the timeout; auth `params` land on the URL and are read at every dial (a refreshed token reaches the next connection); a permission-denied reply emits `permission-denied` and `failed` once; `resyncInterval` re-sends Step1 on the live socket, none without it, and `destroy` clears the timer; `createWebsocketSync` forwards `params`/`WebSocketPolyfill`/`maxBackoffTime` and keys the target on server + room. Retired surface, `it.fails` in 33bb319, each failing for the predicted reason (5): the dial passes the URL alone (reference: `[url, ['edytor-v14']]`); only `synced` fires (reference: the `sync` alias fired `[true]`); no `wsconnecting` field; two same-room providers with no socket do not sync in-process (reference: the BC leg carried `from-a`); `createWebsocketSync` always dials and ignores `connect`/`protocols`/`resyncInterval` (reference: `connect: false` never dialed). **Removed (C1 release notes, 0.0.x API change)**: on `WebsocketProvider` — the `protocols` option and field (pass tokens in `params`), the `sync` event (alias of `synced`), `wsconnecting` (the `status` event carries it), the BroadcastChannel leg: `disableBc`, `bcconnected`, `bcChannel`, `connectBc()`, `disconnectBc()`, `_bcSubscriber` (cross-tab sync is the IndexedDB provider's; the demo already stacks both); on `createWebsocketSync`/`WebsocketSyncOptions` — `connect` (a factory-owned provider with `connect: false` could never be connected), `protocols`, `resyncInterval`, `disableBc`. The `message*` constants still export from `edytor/crdt` (source moved to `room.ts`). **Kept**: `status`/`synced`/`connection-close`/`connection-error`/`permission-denied`/`failed`/`message-error`/`protocol-mismatch`/`schema-mismatch` events, `connect()`/`disconnect()` and the `connect` option, backoff, liveness, `params` (mutable, read at every dial), `awareness`, `WebSocketPolyfill` (the socket seam every test and harness uses). **Resync stayed**, in minimal form (one `setInterval` that sends `room.step1` through the same open-socket guard as all room traffic; default off): it is not a correctness dependency (the join rule heals every reconnect; T2), but the two specs that inject harness loss on a live socket (`dropNext` in `collaboration-websocket*.spec.ts`, plus the rogue-handshake spec that counts refused Step1s) and the collab DST generator (`collab-generator.ts` emits `dropNext`) need a later Step1 to heal a dropped frame, since TCP cannot drop one and no reconnect happens. It is now a provider-class option only (the route's `wsresync`), not a factory option. **Departure**: `disconnect()` sends `room.goodbye` on the socket before closing (before, `disconnectBc` routed it through `broadcast`); F-T9 (websocket-only peer closes its tab) stays green on three engines. **Tests adjusted (D-24 G-e)**: `disableBc: true` dropped from 10 unit-test files, `connect: false` from `f-t7-provider-target.test.ts`'s factory, the `protocols`/`wsconnecting` assertions from `websocket-events.test.ts`; the websocket specs' `bcconnected: false` became `bcLeg: false` (`'bcconnected' in provider`); the test route stops passing `disableBc`. Docs: `crdt-v14-providers.md` (the websocket section lists the retained surface and the retired list), `crdt-v14-browser-proof.md`, README (the factory's `params`/`maxBackoffTime`; stack IndexedDB for cross-tab). **Census**: mechanism counts unchanged; type-body 967 → 961; vendor Δ +27 (unchanged). **Scope note**: `room.ts` was touched beyond the listed files, because the BC leg's removal needed `bcconnected`/`_bcSubscriber` out of `RoomProvider` (now `BcMember`, idb only), `roomChannel` optional, and the goodbye frame shared. The IndexedDB provider is unchanged. **Shortfall**: −68 against ≈−80. DST: not run | **Rows** (tests first; red on the reference as `it.fails`, green after). `src/tests/fixtures/dom/arch-v2-r3-ops.test.tsx` (dom): **F-O5 end to end** — range delete p0@1 → p999@1 and a selected-block delete of 999 blocks through the mounted editor: one change report, no whole-document `project()` while a transaction is open (ref: 2 and 1 — the ops' mid-transaction `flushMirror`), the caret/value right, the command inside a jsdom budget (200 ms; the < 100 ms key-to-frame bound is held in the browser); 2,000 inserts in one transaction: one report naming one block per transaction, no projection, linear (500 vs 2,000 inserts within 8×; ref: quadratic, every `segStart` read the block's uncommitted items). **Operations never read the mirror mid-transaction**: typing, Enter, a merge, a multi-line paste and Tab each make one report and no projection inside the transaction (ref: 1–3 each), with the result caret; a format (pin). **`sel.ride.*`**: a local merge and a write in one transaction — the caret lands at `>alphabeta`@8 and the seam runs 0 times inside the transaction, at most once (ref: once per op inside it); pins: the caret rides two writes of one local transaction (`BCAHello world`@8, the live wrapper), a peer insert before (shifts) and at (stays) the caret, a peer merge of the caret's block (`alphabeta`@7 in the surviving wrapper). `tests/editor-dom/r3-ops.spec.ts` (browsers; green on the reference — D6 already made both deletes one plan): key-to-frame for the two 1,000-paragraph deletes < 100 ms. **Design**. *Operations* read and write only the document (prepare → apply → result): no op site flushes; a caller that needs wrappers after the write gets the op's document result resolved after the commit — `batch(operation, func, prepare, resolve)`: `blockOf` (the inserted, split-born or surviving id of `insertBlockAfter/Before`, `splitBlock`, `mergeBlockBackward/Forward`), `textAfterAtom` (`addInlineBlock` answers the atom id; the resolver finds the text after it and moves the pending marks), `caretOf`/`rangeCaret` (`insertFlow`/`deleteContentWithinSelection` answer `plan.at`). `moveBlock(s)` normalize the destination parent named by the path, not the stale `parent`. *The mirror* is written only by `Edytor.onCommit` → `applyReport(change)` (one editor-lifetime subscription released at `destroy()`): removed ids drop (claimed children kept), each `order` entry goes through the one `reconcileChildren(ids | projected nodes, keep)` (also the initial build from one `project()` at `sync()` and added subtrees), `meta`, then `content` through `reconcileContent` (atoms by id — a pending carrier first — texts by pending slot, else ordinal). The primitives register their `_pendingBlocks`/`_pendingParts` carriers before the write (a write outside a transaction commits at once). *Normalization* runs after the command's commit, not inside its transaction (the order S1's row names: one transaction → normalization): the outermost `Edytor.transact` drains the requests (`Dispatcher.drain`); a pass reads the wrappers the last commit patched, a hook's work is one transaction (`Dispatcher.write`, holding the attempts like any model write) and a re-request is a new pass; the D25 bound moved from the wrapper's recursion depth to the dispatcher (51 passes per normalizer and block per command). Requests carry block ids (`Dispatcher.request`), so the block a flow or a range op leaves the caret in is normalized even when the command created it. A pass that finds nothing opens no transaction (history's open entry keeps the command's result selection). **Deleted** (L16 and the L19 remainder that depended on mid-transaction reads): 15 `flushMirror` call sites and `flushMirror` itself, `_projectedTree`/`_treeCache`, `projectedChildren`, the preflight and full-reconcile fallback of the incremental apply, `ensureFacadeChangeSub` (re-subscribe on attach, converge-after-detach flush), `_mirrorChange`, the duplicate child-reconcile loop, the carrier second pass and the dead `byItems` of `reconcileContent`, `Text.refreshFromProject` and its nine call sites (mid-transaction item refresh in `insertAt`/`deleteAt`/`formatAt`, `markText`, `removeMarksFromText`, the observer's adoption, `refreshSelectionTextFromModel`, `refreshFromModel`), `Block._normalizationDepth` and the per-wrapper recursion cap, **`Edytor.mirrorRevision`** (the selection's compatibility cache keys on `_docCommitVersion`, the existing — previously write-only — commit counter, bumped after the patch: one counter, not two), the dead `Text({segment})` and `Block({projected})` constructor modes, `Block.displayLength` (no caller), the document-reading half of `Block.textAtOffset` (it maps over the committed parts) and `edytor.utils`' duplicate `textAt`. **Census** (vendor excluded): **`flushMirror(` 15 → 0**, **`mirrorRevision` 2 → 0**; setTimeout 24, rAF 8, `await tick()` 12, `stopCapturing()` 1, `instanceof PreventionError` 1, `ignoreNextSelectionChange` writes 7, MutationObserver 4, `flushSync(` 0 — unchanged; type-body 969; vendor Δ +27. **Perf** (rule 10). `bench:crdt` p50, before → after (two after-runs; the bench imports only `src/lib/crdt`, which R3 does not touch — the spread is machine load, v13 moved with it): keystroke 1k blocks facade 0.500 → 0.623/0.623 ms (write 0.044 → 0.063, commit 0.032 → 0.043), runs 0.014 → 0.026/0.023 ms, 37 B unchanged; typing local 0.0201 → 0.0179/0.0201 ms; load 1k 2.02 → 2.12/2.48 ms; delta 128 runs 0.059 → 0.070/0.064 ms; seam/caret 33/35 B unchanged. **1,000-block delete probe** (the readers' 10.6 s through the view vs 7 ms on the document; a temporary uncommitted probe in the dom lane, and the chromium spec): document work range/blocks 12.6/11.7 → 9.8/12.9 ms; through the view, jsdom command 37/52 → 39/62 ms (+ jsdom's render of 999 removals ≈ 125 ms, unchanged); chromium key-to-frame 34–40/30–35 → 36–38/30–33 ms; firefox/webkit after 59–60/50–69 ms. The 10.6 s was gone before R3: D6 made both deletes one plan, which left one mid-transaction whole-tree reconcile per command; R3 removes that and the per-op project/reconcile everywhere else (typing, Enter, merge, paste, Tab), which the rows count rather than time. **Tests rewritten** (mechanism pins, test-only): `scoped-text-refresh` drops the `flushMirror`/fallback counters and the `refreshFromProject` calls — its mid-transaction rows now assert that the document shows the writes while the wrapper shows what was committed, and the commit's report updates it; the D25 normalization rows assert the per-command pass limit and that the next command is not suppressed (instead of `_normalizationDepth === 0`); a comment in `command-simulation`. `mirror-incremental` is kept (it pins the `_pendingBlocks` registry, L17/R4). **Where the step went** (plan −385 = L16 315 + L19's remainder): taken ≈ −420 gross (the flush sites and the flush, the tree memo, the fallback and preflight, the re-subscription, the refresh, the second pass and `byItems`, the depth cap, the dead modes); new code — the post-commit drain and pass limit in `session/commands.ts` +36 net, the result resolvers (`blockOf`, `textAfterAtom`, `caretOf`, `rangeCaret`, `batch`'s resolve) ≈ +45, `applyReport`'s shared reconcile ≈ +10 → −299. Left for R4 (L17): `_pendingBlocks`/`_pendingParts` adoption, the `_live`/`_bound`/`_blockId` flags and the `get(id) === this` guards (positional text ids are still the wrappers' own ids), `projectedParts`/`partOffsetOf`/`atomOffsetOfPartIndex` (the part-index vocabulary the primitives write with — `segStart` reads the document so a primitive after a write in the same transaction lands right; segment 0 answers 0 without a read). **Deviations**: (1) Normalization after the commit (above) instead of at the end of the command's transaction: a command whose normalization writes makes two reports and two updates (the op's, then the pass's, merged into one undo step); a peer can see the pre-normalization state for one update (an emptied root before its fresh paragraph). (2) The dom F-O5 rows time the command against a 200 ms jsdom budget; the row's < 100 ms end to end is the chromium spec (the compare pass it also counts is R6's). (3) `r2-render`'s "the first character typed into an empty block keeps the text element" failed in 2 of 13 full dom lanes (once with the temporary 1,000-paragraph probe running beside it, once without), never alone (10/10) and not in seven instrumented lanes that logged every remount — reported as a load flake, cause not found; the reference was not run 13 times to compare. **Design iterations**: one failed — normalization first ran each pass inside its own transaction, which opened a history transaction even for a pass with no work and closed the command's open selection entry (redo restored the pre-split caret: `dom.test.ts` undo/redo rows); passes now open a transaction only for a hook's work. **Contract questions**: (a) Normalization after the command's commit (two updates when it writes) — confirm against S1's "one transaction → normalization"; the alternative keeps it inside the transaction and needs extension hooks that read the document (R4's handles). (b) An operation's wrapper result is resolved after the commit: called inside an outer transaction (`edytor.transact(() => block.splitBlock(…))`) it answers what the committed wrappers hold — `null` for a block the transaction created. K5 note for 0.0.x (`splitBlock`, `insertBlockAfter/Before`, `mergeBlock*`, `addInlineBlock`), or should nested calls be refused? (c) A vetoed `insertFlow` now answers `[null, 0]` instead of `undefined` (callers destructure it). |

DST: not run | **Rows** (tests first, red on the reference as `it.fails`/`test.fails`, green after). `src/tests/crdt/arch-v2/r4-handles.test.ts` (crdt lane, headless view): **one command, one update** (orchestrator requirement 1) — `[a]`, remove `a`: exactly one `update`, and a peer applying it sees `[paragraph]`, never an empty root (ref: 2 updates, the root's default child in its own); a `note` kind whose `normalizeContent` turns `x` into `y`, `setBlock` to `axb`: one update, `ayb`, one undo step (ref: 2 updates); pin: a normalizer that finds nothing writes nothing. **Op results inside an outer transaction** (orchestrator requirement 2) — `edytor.transact(() => a.splitBlock(…))` answers the split-born block, whose type and text read in the transaction (ref: `null`); `insertBlockAfter`/`insertBlockBefore` answer the inserted blocks; `mergeBlockBackward` answers a surviving block the same transaction created; `addInlineBlock` answers the text after the atom (ref: `undefined`/`null` for all four). **Handles read the index** — mid-transaction `firstText.stringContent` shows a model write and `children` a child inserted in the transaction (R4b); `insertChildren`/`insertParts` take JSON specs (R4a); pins: one handle per live id across commits and for a block a peer added; a handle for a removed id answers `isInTree === false` and the cache no longer resolves it. `src/tests/fixtures/dom/arch-v2-r4-views.test.tsx` (dom, R4a): a block snippet receives `{id, type, data, selected, focused}` — not a `Block` — whose `data` follows a committed `setBlock`, with `handle` the block's handle; an inline-atom snippet receives `{id, type, data, selected}` — not an `InlineBlock`; pin: `use:block.attach` still registers the element. **Plugin fixtures migrated** (the §9.3 R4 tests): the normalization contracts fixtures replace content with JSON parts (`insertParts(0, [{type: 'mention'}, …])`, `[[{text: 'Hello'}], [{text: ' world', marks}]]`), the pipeline carrier fixture inserts a JSON part (it joins the live segment; no second segment resolves), the code plugin's line-splitting normalizer inserts `{type: 'codeLine', content}` specs. **Design**. *R4a (L17)*: specs are data — `Block.insertChildren(index, (JSONBlock | Block)[])` creates a spec through `jsonBlockToSpec` (a `Block` moves with its identity), `insertParts(index, (JSONText[] | JSONInlineBlock)[])` writes JSON parts at the part's display start; `addChildBlock(s)`, the root's default child, `clear()` and `acceptSuggestedText` pass JSON; `suggestText` groups text runs without sentinel texts. Snippet view objects (`BlockView`, `InlineBlockView` in `plugins.ts`): getters over the cell (`type`, `data`: reactive per cell) and the selection sets (`selected`, `focused`), plus `handle`, `attach`, `void` (R5 moves the element into the core); a suggestion's atom answers `selected: false`. *R4b (handles, `session/handles.ts`)*: `Handles` is the view's registry (`edytor.idToBlock`): `get(id)` answers a live block's handle (`isVisibleBlock`, transaction-aware), `block(id)` the handle live or not, `text(block, ordinal)`, `atom(block, atom)` (the block that renders it is recorded, so a merged atom follows), `textById`, `parts(block)` — a block's segments and atoms with display starts, from `facade.runs(block)` (read-your-writes, cached per block by the index), memoized on the runs array; `prune(change)` drops the handles of `change.removed` at each commit. `Block`/`Text`/`InlineBlock` keep their public surface as getters (`parent` via `positionOf`, `children` via `childrenIds`, `content` via `parts`, `type`/`data` via `blockTypeOf`/`blockDataOf`, `index`, `isInTree`; `Text.segStart`/`length`/`value`/`stringContent`/`isInDocument` from its segment; atom `type`/`data`/`isInDocument`); mutators are unchanged (commands through the dispatcher; `insertAt`/`deleteAt`/`formatAt` write at `segStart + offset`, read from the index so a write after another in one transaction lands right). *Normalization inside the transaction*: the outermost `Edytor.transact` drains the dispatcher's requests at the end of its callback, inside the same `doc.transact`; `Dispatcher.write` runs a hook's work in that transaction; the D25 pass limit is unchanged. Op results: `blockOf`/`textAfterAtom`/`caretOf` resolve through the handles, so they answer mid-transaction. The commit (`onCommit`) now prunes handles, marks the DOM churn once when content changed or blocks were removed, and repairs a selection that no longer resolves. The selection's compatibility cache keys on the projection (per (value, index version)) and the Surface fields only: handles are live, so there is no second counter. **Deleted**. R4a: L17 — `new Block({block})` (detached and JSON-root constructor modes), `new Text({parent, content})`'s content buffer, `new InlineBlock({block})`, `_pendingBlocks`, `_pendingParts` and the carrier slots of `reconcileContent`, `_bind`'s adoption rename, `_toSpec`, `spliceTextItems`, `formatTextItems`, `_sliceFrom`, the unbound branches of `insertAt`/`deleteAt`/`formatAt`/`insertChildren`/`deleteChildren`/`insertParts`/`removeMarksFromText`, `deleteParts`, `InlineBlock._spec` and its `type` setter, `groupContent`, `_bound`, the `jsonBlockToSpec` re-export. R4b: the wrapper mirror's remainder — `applyReport`, `reconcileChildren`, `reconcileContent`, `_reconcile`, `_reconcileMeta`, `_drop`, `_bind`, `Text._bind`/`_kill`/`_setItems`/`_items`/`_segOrd`/`_live`, `InlineBlock._bindRun`/`_kill`/`_live`, `Block._live`/`_blockId`, every `get(id) === this` guard on the id registries (`idToBlock`, `idToText`, `idToInlineBlock`; the node registry's stays), `idToInlineBlock`, the `idToText` attachment registry (now a lookup of live segments), the eager `$state` copies (`children`, `content`, `data`, `#type`, `definition`, `index`), `projectedParts`, `atomOffsetOfPartIndex`, `partOffsetOf` (its seven callers read `text.segStart`), `deriveContentParts`/`displayLenOfPart` (one `contentParts` in `session/handles.ts`), `Edytor._docCommitVersion`. **Census** (vendor excluded): unchanged — setTimeout 24, rAF 8, `await tick()` 12, `flushMirror(` 0, `stopCapturing()` 1, `instanceof PreventionError` 1, `ignoreNextSelectionChange` writes 7, MutationObserver 4, `flushSync(` 0; type-body 969 → 982 (+13: the `BlockView`/`InlineBlockView` declarations); vendor Δ +27. **K5 migration notes** (0.0.x API changes, recorded here for the C1 release notes): (1) specs are data: `new Block({block})`, `new Text({…content})`, `new InlineBlock({block})` are gone — `block.insertChildren(index, JSONBlock[] | Block[])` (a handle moves), `block.insertParts(index, (JSONText[] | JSONInlineBlock)[])`; `deleteParts` removed (use `model.deleteText`/`removeInline` or `deleteContentAtRange`). (2) `Block`, `Text`, `InlineBlock` are id-only handles: getters read the document (inside a command they show its writes), they are **not reactive** — a template reads its snippet's view object or the cells; `edytor.idToBlock` is the handle registry (`get(id)` answers live blocks only). (3) `Text` has no identity across commits: it is the `ordinal`-th segment of `blockId` (`text.id` = `t:<block>:<ordinal>`); key extension state by block id + anchor. (4) Liveness is `block.isInTree`, `text.isInDocument`, `atom.isInDocument`; `_live`, `_bound`, `_blockId`, `_segOrd`, `_items` are gone; a dead block's `parent` is `undefined`. (5) `edytor.idToText` resolves live segments (`get` only), `edytor.idToInlineBlock` is removed (`nodeToText`/`nodeToInlineBlock` stay). (6) Block snippets receive `block: BlockView` = `{id, type, data, selected, focused, handle, attach, void}`, inline-atom snippets `block: InlineBlockView` = `{id, type, data, selected, handle, attach}`; commands and document reads go through `block.handle`. (7) `normalizeContent`/`normalizeChildren` run at the end of the command's transaction, inside it, with a handle that reads the command's writes; returned work runs in the same transaction (the doc comment that told extensions to read Yjs types directly is replaced). (8) `splitBlock`, `insertBlockAfter/Before`, `mergeBlock*`, `addInlineBlock`, `addChildBlock(s)` answer handles of the blocks they created, inside an outer transaction too (R3's contract question (b) answered: no refusal, a handle). (9) `Block.partOffsetOf`/`atomOffsetOfPartIndex`/`projectedParts` removed: a text's display offset is `text.segStart`, a block's parts `edytor.idToBlock.parts(id)`. (10) `InlineBlock.type` has no setter; `Block.type`'s setter writes the document only. (11) `suggestText` stores `[atom, [text runs]]` without empty sentinel texts. **Tests rewritten** (test-only): `scoped-text-refresh` (a text handle shows the writes inside a transaction; the 0-`project()` rows keep; sibling untouched via `blockVersion`), `mirror-incremental` (the handle tree equals the projection after every local and remote commit; `_pendingBlocks` invariants dropped at R4a), `attachmentLifecycle` (`idToText` resolves a live segment without an element), the pipeline carrier fixture, the suggestion grouping fixture (2 groups), `elegance-selection` (the string-id caret-write case removed — that form went with R2), `inline-atomic.spec.ts:1025` (the `idToInlineBlock.size` assertion dropped), and `_live`/`_blockId` reads in the dom fixtures, `collaboration-websocket.spec`, `composition-remote-lock.spec`, the test route and the DST collab runner/selection oracle (`isInDocument`/`isInTree`). **Where the step went** (plan −315 = L17 345 − handles ≈30): L17 taken whole at R4a (−330 net, views +37 included); R4b removed the mirror's remainder that R3 left (≈−270 gross: reconcile/drop/bind/kill, flags, eager state, offset helpers) against `session/handles.ts` +91 → −179. **Deviations**: (1) Split R4a/R4b (rule 6), above. (2) R3's F-O5 row "2,000 inserts in one transaction … linear" now times the view's work outside the index's `runs` reads: a text handle reads the index, whose mid-transaction read re-walks the block's uncommitted (unmerged) items — O(items) per read — so the loop as a whole is superlinear again (node probe, 1,000 paragraphs: 500 inserts 45 ms vs 34 ms at R3, 2,000 inserts 384 ms vs 208 ms; the index's reads are 17 ms and 181 ms of that); the view's own time stays linear and the row's other claims (one report, one block named, no projection) are unchanged. (3) A dead block's handle keeps the parts it last read (`Handles.parts`) — the write intent (`#intent`) of a text that died to a merge is minted from its last display, as the mirror kept it. (4) `edytor.idToBlock` keeps its name for the `Handles` registry (no churn across 40 call sites). (5) The DEV `rendersContent` check runs for every rendered block (it ran only while a wrapper was registered). **Design iterations**: none failed. On the way: parts first came from `facade.contentItems` memoized on the index version, which re-derived every block after any write; they now come from the per-block `runs` cache (same cost for the written block, none for the others). **Contract questions**: (a) F-O5's linearity with handles reading the index mid-transaction — is the index's mid-transaction read O(unmerged items) acceptable (D12's stream switch may change it), or should the row keep timing the whole loop and the index fold runs incrementally per block? (b) A text handle's pending marks (`markOnNextInsert`) stay with (block, ordinal), as the wrapper's did — confirm, or move them to the selection with S3's `marksForInsertion`. (c) View objects carry `handle` as their command surface instead of enumerated command helpers — confirm the shape for R5. (d) Normalization when the outermost transaction is not the view's (an outsider's `doc.transact`): the requests drain at the end of the view's own outermost `transact` call, inside the outsider's transaction — confirm. **Answers** (orchestrator): (a) accepted for now — the per-block incremental fold is deferred to D12 (the stream table joins the index) or the C1 perf pass; F-O5 keeps its view-only timing with this note. (b) Pending marks move to the selection value (plan L4) at R5, not in R4. (c) Yes: view objects expose `handle` as the command surface. (d) Confirmed. |

per-stream delete and format over boundary-free pieces, right to left; split = one `insertAtGapEnd` + the claims after the split point moved; claim append; renderer-free writes), `readRange` skipping boundaries, a renderer-free `RangeCursor`, and `ownText`. The **stream table joins the D9 index** (`text/runs.ts`): one scanned row per backing text; an edit rescans only its text; a changed boundary set, nonce, own text or registry entry rebuilds the delimiters and streams and invalidates the blocks whose stream identity or text layout changed; otherwise only the edited text's streams move and the edit invalidates the display owners of the streams its inserted spans touch (L7 kept as that narrowing; a delete, a format or an opaque span invalidates every reader of the text). The facade's anchor codec: `anchorAt` locates the display offset in the block's pieces (a hidden block's own pieces too) and mints an engine relative position into that text (`b` = its home); `resolveAnchor` finds the stream holding the resolved index and its display owner, else `null` (seam). A streamless block's first typing or split first calls `ownText`: its `content` attr and a re-minted `n` are written in the op's transaction by a writer id `hash32(schema, block id, dead n, node / nonce / content item ids)` (the seed writer's hashed-writer mechanism, written in place: `doc.clientID` is swapped for the two map writes, so replicas that type first concurrently write identical items and both typings land in one text), the nonce derived from the same hash; the attribution record's incarnation moves with it (`BA.retarget`), and the facade's UndoManager drops those items from its stack items (a per-doc local id set), so no undo step removes a text two replicas share. Migration `restoreBlocks` writes a fresh own text and an empty claims list (re-minting `n` for a block whose stream starts at a live boundary). The claims attr is `claims` (plan §2.1); **schema generation 3 → 4**. **Deleted**: L1 (slice payloads, `readSliceEntries`, record anchors with B/E sentinels, `sliceRange`), L2 (interval index, lazy-expiry heap sweep, `gatherClaims`, `computeOwnership`'s contest, `maxG`, `claimRoutesToB`), L3 (empty-display revive, left-edge rewrite, right-edge rival claim), L4 (`materializeSegs`, `splitSlices`), L5 (`undo-repair.ts`, `undoRepairClaims`, its attach), L6 (`recordsByText`, text effects, atom rows, `rangeCache`, the claim-fingerprint churn refinement), L7 (the item-extent narrowing against interval rows — replaced by stream spans), L8 (`a: -2` + `o`, `emissionOffset`, the outward scan), the D11 spike `streams.next.ts` and the test backend switch (`backend.ts`, `stream-ops.ts`, `d11-scenarios-streams.test.ts` — the corpus runs on production in `scenarios.test.ts`). **Re-pins (YATA order, lower client id left)**: **TX06a** (= F-D14) and **TX09a** (orchestrator answer 1) in `scenarios/active-text.ts`, three assignments each; F-U4d (D-17) as pinned at D11, now on production; TX04a holds unchanged. **Tests rewritten**: oracles (`fresh-view`, `runs`, `model-ops`) and the harness adapter (`classifyTagAtoms`/`tagAtomDeps`/`captureOpState`/`opTarget`: a position's owner is its stream's display owner, a boundary is the claim a split writes) and the corpus runner's split/insert envelopes (a split writes one boundary and moves merge claims from any list routing to the target; typing writes no claim); anchor rows lost the owner facet; regression B's ownership rows (typing at an emptied head lands before the tail's boundary); `gateH-R1` masks boundary nonces in its delta differential; `wu6-delete-range` restaged with legal ops (splits, then two merges back) instead of registry removal; `shared-state` compares the stream table with a fresh one. **Deleted tests** (slice mechanisms): `text/ownership-intervals`, `gateF2/wu6-interval-fuzz`, `hardening/d6-undo-repair-key`, `harness/dense-ownership-oracle`, WU1 probes 3 and 6 and regression A's disjoint-coverage row (all staged fragmented slice coverage by deleting registry entries — a state streams do not have). The DOM test route's `probeSliceRanges` became `probeStreams` (DST collab diagnostics; not run). **Census** (vendor excluded): mechanism counts unchanged (setTimeout 24, rAF 8, `await tick()` 12, `flushMirror(` 0, `stopCapturing()` 1, `instanceof PreventionError` 1, `ignoreNextSelectionChange` writes 7, MutationObserver 4, `flushSync(` 0); type-body 969 → 921. **Vendor delta**: +27 (P7), unchanged — D12 adds no engine code (boundaries stay embedded ContentAny items, §11.5 not taken). **Bench** (`bench:crdt` p50, before → after (two runs)): typing local 0.0185 → 0.0187 / 0.0185 ms; keystroke 1k blocks facade 0.655 → 0.532 / 0.503 ms (write 0.066 → 0.071 / 0.061, commit 0.047 → 0.038 / 0.031; the fresh ownership stage 0.955 → 0.290 / 0.263 ms), runs 0.024 → 0.024 / 0.020 ms, 37 B unchanged; load 1k 2.12 → 2.24 / 2.15 ms (v13, untouched, 1.84 → 1.83 / 1.91 — noise); delta 128 runs 0.063 → 0.063 / 0.061 ms; split 100k 345 → 230 B; merge 35 → 36 B; seam 35 B / 0.033 ms → 35 B / 0.019 ms, caret 33 B / 0.040 ms → 35 B / 0.019 ms. Shared text (the U8b probe: 50 streams of one 100k text): keystroke p50 0.282 → 0.171 ms, one recompute; `formatRange` 1.04 → 1.13 ms (opaque on both: every reader recomputes). Requirement 6 met: neither the default path nor the switched path is worse. **Deviations**: (1) The own text is written in place under a derived writer id (a clientID swap for two map writes inside the command's transaction), not by applying a scratch doc's update: a scratch doc cannot address an existing node's attrs without the whole document, and the command's transaction is open on every editor path; identical ids imply identical items because the hash covers the node, nonce and content item ids. (2) Raw `Y.UndoManager`s (the harness) still capture those items — convergent, but their undo removes the shared text; only the facade's manager filters them. (3) Several live boundaries naming one block with the matching nonce (concurrent undo/redo copies): the lowest item id delimits — the spike took the first per text in registry iteration order, which is replica-dependent across texts. (4) `anchorAt` also mints in hidden blocks (their own pieces) so a caret write aimed at a text that died to a merge follows its items (`arch-v2-v3-seam` V3 row); a streamless block's anchor is `{b: block, a: {i: null}}`, the start of the text its first typing creates. (5) The claims attr was renamed `slices` → `claims` with the generation bump. **Contract questions**: (a) the derived-writer own text, written in place and kept out of the facade's history (deviations 1–2) — acceptable as the seed-writer mechanism? (b) split of a streamless block at 0 ‖ first typing into it: YATA order (the TX09a class) — pin it with D-1? (c) the lowest-id delimiter rule (deviation 3). (d) migration `force` re-mints `n` for a boundary-delimited block without moving its attribution record (restore writes no attribution): its `createdBy` is replaced at the next attributed write. (e) boundaries carry the new block's random nonce in the text, so two replicas running one schedule no longer have equal backing-text deltas (the `gateH-R1` differential masks it); a seeded `rand` seam makes them equal. (f) `contentTarget` now admits a live block without its own text (a streamless block gets one on first write). **Answers (orchestrator, D12 follow-up)**: (a) the in-place derived writer (a temporary clientID swap inside the command's transaction, kept out of the facade's history) is accepted as the seed-writer mechanism on the owned fork; (b) a split at 0 of a textless block concurrent with a first typing follows YATA order under D-1; (c) lowest item id for duplicate boundaries: accepted; (d) a forced re-migration's new nonce losing `createdBy`: accepted — listed for the C1 release notes; (e) random nonces making raw backing-text deltas differ: accepted. |

R4 measured a red seed in 3 of 4 file runs on its reference, whose placeholder repair ran a real 1000 ms timer. **K5 migration notes**: (1) `placeholder` (prop, options, plugin) is `string \| (view: {type, data, focused, empty}) => string \| null`; the snippet form is gone. It renders as `data-placeholder` on the empty `[data-edytor-text]` with the library's `::before` rule (absolute, so the caret stays at the start); style it with `[data-placeholder]::before`. `[data-edytor-text-placeholder]`, its `role=button` and click handlers are gone — clicking the row places the caret natively. (2) Snippets render inner markup only: a kind declares `element` (`'li'`, `{tag, attributes}`, or `data => …`; default `div`) and `viewState` (attributes the browser owns, `['open']` on details/toggle); `snippet` is optional (a `hr` kind has none); `use:block.attach` is removed (`BlockView.attach` gone) — the core renders, registers and marks void the element, and `onBlockAttached` runs once per element; `use:block.void` still marks inner chrome. Inline atoms: the core wrapper is the atom's one element (`InlineBlockView.attach` gone; `handle` is `undefined` for a suggested atom). (3) `Text.markOnNextInsert` is gone: pending marks are `SelectionValue.pending`, read with `edytor.selection.pending`, written with `selection.stage(marks)` (`undefined` clears); a caret that did not move keeps them, a move clears them, an insertion at the caret consumes them, an atom inserted at the caret carries them after it. (4) `Block.suggestions` returns the JSON parts (`rawSuggestions` kept as an alias); the readonly Proxy wrappers are gone. (5) `edytor.placeholderRepair` is gone; `edytor.placeholderAt(id)` and `edytor.overlay` (`layer`, `add(measure)`, `invalidate()`) are new. (6) Chrome DOM: handle hosts live in `[data-edytor-overlay]` (a sibling after the host) in document order, not before each block inside `[data-edytor]`; `--edytor-handle-offset-y` is gone; the drop indicator is in the overlay (`position: absolute`), not `document.body`; remote carets are layer-relative; menus keep `position: fixed` inside the layer. (7) Block identity attributes are declarative, so they are in server-rendered HTML: "a `[data-edytor-block]` exists" no longer means hydrated (the browser helper waits for a registered text element). **Where the step went** (plan −645 ≈ L34 −350, L48 −100, L49 −100, L50 −81; pending marks ±0): L34 −397 (queue −192, Text −114, observer −58, selection −26, events −7), L48 ≈ −106 (Proxies −118, view values +12), L49 ≈ −59 (snippets −57, block handle −26, core element +17, plugin types +7; the handle ownership guard went with the plugin rewrite, counted under L50; the root-attribute action was not taken, deviation 3), L50 −79 (handles plugin −174 − 18 + 93, overlay +50, menus −22, remote −8), R5 wiring +16 (`placeholderAt`, overlay mount, readonly invalidation). **Deviations**: (1) Split R5a/R5b/R5c (rule 6). (2) The declared mark elements (the ten rich-text mark snippets, `dom-mutation.spec`'s mark specs) are not in this checkpoint — outside the orchestrator's R5 scope and its −645 (the plan's −670 includes them). (3) The root-attribute action stays: a declarative `autocorrect` goes through Svelte's property path, and `HTMLElement.autocorrect` is a boolean IDL attribute in current engines (it would write "on"); needs a browser check first. (4) The handles plugin's WeakSet (the component's default-plugin detection) stays: it is composition, not ownership. (5) The observer's `data-edytor-plugin-chrome` tolerance stays for R7 — no core chrome is inside the host any more. (6) `viewState` is declared but not consumed yet: block elements are tolerant surfaces; R7's ownership table reads it. (7) Test infra updated with the DOM shape in the implementation commits: `getPlaceholderLocators` (the placeholder's row), `waitForEditorReady`, specs that clicked the placeholder button by role, the `unsupported-beforeinput` block text, the input specs that now wait for the click's selection, `inline-atomic` mapping count 2 → 1 (one element per atom), demo specs' handle-host locators, the T6 caret row's frame wait. (8) The visible set: a handle whose block is more than a viewport away keeps its last place (no IntersectionObserver); a never-placed handle is measured once and stays hidden until then. **Design iterations**: none failed. On the way: the drop indicator first appeared at the layer's origin for one frame (a spec caught it) — it is now placed when shown, then per frame. **Contract questions**: (a) The placeholder function runs only for blocks that show one, so `empty` is always true — keep D-8's view shape, or call it for every single-text block? (b) The overlay's visible-set rule (skip beyond a viewport; one layout read per frame for the origin) against R6's p95 keystroke gate at 5k blocks — accept, or add an IntersectionObserver? (c) Retire in-host `data-edytor-plugin-chrome` at R7? (d) AGENTS.md's block-handle paragraph (shared resize and structural observers, host before the block) is stale — C1, or now? |

7 red on the reference as `it.fails`): F-I12 (a) D-20 **delete** (`prev \| abc\|`, preview `に`, the peer deletes the block → the session ends inside the sync, `['paragraph:prev']` on both replicas, the IME's later `compositionend` writes nothing, caret in `prev`), **retype** (→ `quote:abcに`, caret 4, one undo → `quote:abc`), **re-parent** (nest → `prev{abcに}`), **merge** (→ `prevabcに`, caret 8); F-I12 (b) the IME's next update after the forced commit opens a new session over the committed `に` → `abc日本` once on both replicas; F-I15 ×2 (the filler node before the first preview is the node after release, holding `す`; no placeholder while live; typing `a` into an empty block and deleting it back keeps the node). Pins (green on the reference): F-I10 (`ab@c\|d`, a peer atom before `@`: the composing text nodes stay, commit lands after `@`), F-I10b (`ab@cd\|`, a peer mention between `c` and `d`: `c`, `d` shown once, the atom after the commit, `ab \| c \| dに`), F-I11 (`ab@\|cd`, the peer deletes `@`: the node survives, `abにcd` in one text element after), a peer split inside the composing block is not structural (the session lives, F-I21 unchanged), Chromium's plain-insertText commit after a retype lands once / after a delete writes nothing (green in jsdom on the reference: its session was never ended), a canceled composition in an empty block brings the placeholder back. cdp (`tests/editor-dom/arch-v2-i4-pin.cdp.spec.ts`, 8 tests, peer in a second context over the relay): F-I10, F-I10b, F-I11 (session contract: IME node kept, one `compositionstart`/`compositionend`, no `Selection` write while live; outcome converged) **green on the reference** (R2's frozen segment list); F-I12 (a) delete/retype/re-parent/merge and (b) were `test.fail` on the reference (the session stayed live through the change; on retype the IME's next update landed at offset 0: `にbetan`) — **flipped green**. `ime-baseline.cdp.spec.ts` F-I15 **flipped green** (its `knownRed` removed). **Design**. *One node*: `Text.svelte` renders `{#each empty ? FILLER : deltas}` with `FILLER = [{text: '​', marks: []}]` — the filler is the same keyed item (`0:[]`) as the first unmarked delta, so the empty → non-empty change is a `nodeValue` write on the node the IME wrote into, never a branch swap (BI-15); a first delta with marks still renders its `Mark` element. *D-20* (`session/composition`, the session's structural-change rule; `surface/pin` stays the only freeze mechanism): `start()` records where the host's block sits (`#where`: its id and its ancestors', with their types); `Edytor.onCommit` calls `composition.restructured(change)` after the prune and the dead-endpoint repair; when the report names a removal, move or meta change and the place differs (not visible — deleted or merged away —, re-parented itself or through an ancestor, retyped itself or an ancestor), the session ends **inside the report**: the preview still live and whole in one visible block (`#kept`: both region anchors resolve in the same visible block, `preview.length` apart) is committed with what the IME shows (`pin.imeBuffer() ?? preview`, an ordinary `#end` — hooks see one insertion, one undo step with the session); a preview deleted with its block is dropped without a write. The commit's transaction is queued by the engine behind the current report (Yjs runs a transaction opened in an `update` handler after the current cleanup), so every subscriber sees the structural report first and the commit's report next, both before Svelte's flush: the structural change never renders with the session live. A sibling-index shift and a peer split are not structural for the host. *Continuation*: Chromium drops its composition when its node goes (no `compositionend`) and delivers the IME's commit as a plain `insertText` (observed on cdp: `betann` / `ngamma` before the rule). After a D-20 commit the session keeps `#resume` = the committed range (or `lost` after a deletion) until a non-composition occurrence other than a plain `insertText`, a pointer gesture, focus loss or a `compositionend`/cancel; the IME's next `compositionstart`, update or commit starts a session over it (its replacement goes through the ordinary start-target command), and a `lost` resume starts a refused session (writes nothing; the tail restores the host). **Deviations**: (1) plan Δ +25 → +43: the continuation (`#resume`, +14) is needed for F-I12 (b) on the real IME path, which the Δ did not price. (2) `ime-baseline` `composeObserved` strips the empty filler from `liveDom` — the filler now shares the IME's node, so the live node reads `す​` (harness change, as I3's F-I16 (d)). (3) Two dom pins were added in the implementation commit (Chromium's plain-commit shape), green on the reference in jsdom. (4) Writing the D-20 commit from an `onChange` subscriber contradicts the facade doc's "No writes, ever" reading for subscribers; it relies on the engine's queued cleanup order (no crdt change made). **Load flake**: `arch-v2-r2-render` "a format over a range keeps the text element" failed in 4 of 19 full `test:dom` runs on the candidate (1 of 8 with I4's `Text.svelte` reverted, 0 of 8 on the reference): the legacy observer's `hasManagedSubtreeMutation` classifies the format's own `Mark` insertion as foreign damage and remounts (`refreshFromModel` from `domTextMutationObserver.ts:1774`), only under load (load average 11–19 while the D12 merge ran); green 3/3 alone. Not attributed to one change; R6's compare pass retires the path. **Census** (vendor excluded): unchanged — setTimeout 20, rAF 5, `await tick()` 12, `flushMirror(` 0, `stopCapturing()` 1, `instanceof PreventionError` 1, `ignoreNextSelectionChange` writes 0, MutationObserver 4, `flushSync(` 0; no new timer, flag window or retry. **API**: `Edytor.composition.restructured(change)` (new), `occurred(inputType?)`. **Design iterations**: one — the first cut cleared the resume on every non-composition `beforeinput`, so Chromium's plain `insertText` commit after the forced commit duplicated (`betann`, `ngamma`); a plain `insertText` now keeps it. **Fix (coordinator answers)**: (a) a same-parent reorder is a D-20 trigger — `start()` also records each chain member's parent's child ids; a report that changes a member's rank among the siblings it had and still has commits first (an insertion or removal around it, an index shift only, does not: pinned). Rows: dom "a peer moves the composing block after its next sibling: committed first, once" (red on 0cd6633: the session stayed live) + the continuation `abc日本` on both replicas, and the index-shift pin; cdp F-I12 (a) reorder and (b) reorder (plus (b) for re-parent and merge). **Chromium does not keep the composition through the same-parent move**: with the trigger removed, (a) kept the session live and (b) duplicated the continuation into the next block (`ににgamma`, `betan` left behind) — the rule is needed, and passes. (b) accepted: a deletion before the session's first write starts the continuation at the seam caret. (c) accepted as ONE named exception: `crdt/edytor-doc.ts` `onChange`'s "No writes, ever" now names the D-20 commit and why it is safe (the engine queues a transaction opened in an `update` handler until the current one finishes, so every listener sees the structural report first). |

(3) `extension-markup-removed` **24** → **D-25 / §6 D59, F-O13 (c)**: today removes nodes in a kind's own markup around the slots — including the callout icon a snippet renders after a slash/markdown retype (a live defect) and foreign nodes beside a text element (two gate3 specs expect that removal). (4) `claimed-drift-restored-by-render` **21** and (5) `structure-restored-by-render` **22** → **F-O13 (d)** / §1.3: drift an expectation claims (model-owned attempt, tail, a structural key without `beforeinput`), or structure the browser changed around model-owned input, that the command's own render restored; same outcome, nothing left for today's observer. (6) `edit-lost-to-same-task-render` **7** → **F-O13 (b), (e)**: a browser edit plus a same-task model change: the flush overwrites the edit and today never adopts it; the snapshot places it (only in the R6 rows — no existing spec does this). (7) `missing-element-left-by-today` **4** → **BI2-2, L39** (R7): a registered element gone while its cell renders it — a code line vanishes from the DOM after Enter in a code block on a hydrated `/test/dom` page after earlier scenarios (`cells-shadow.spec` "marks, inline atoms, lists and code"; reproduced on the reference: model `constlet x = 2;`, ` a = 1;`, `return a;`, DOM without the second line) and after Android's native paragraph in a code line (mobile-webkit, intermittent); nothing restores it today. (8) `foreign-text-in-strict-container` **4** → **BI2-2** (R7): a foreign element with text inside a text element: the location default adopts its text and removes it; today removes both. (9) `residue-after-space-normalization` **3** → **F-O10**: after today's WebKit converted-space normalization the DOM keeps one space more than the cell. (10) `attribute-heal-does-not-converge` **2** → **F-P17 (e)**: a foreign writer re-damaging every heal outlasts the bounded repair. (11) `attribute-divergence-resolved-by-render` **1** → **F-O2 / R11**: WebKit leaves `style=""` where it rejects the core's `user-select` on a void block; the table would strip it, the element went first. **Found by the shadow and fixed here** (small, behaviour-correcting): (a) `data-edytor-selected`/`-focused` were written imperatively by `select()` on `block.node` and lost whenever a block element was re-created (retype, re-parent, split) — 100+ attribute divergences in the first census; `Block.svelte` renders them (R11, F-O2); (b) Chromium serializes `white-space` as `white-space-collapse` + `text-wrap-mode`, so the strict style strip re-healed every text element on every attribute record (a heal loop per keystroke); the longhands are owned; (c) `contenteditable` on atoms, void chrome and text in voids is set as an attribute (jsdom does not reflect the property). **K2 latency gate** (`node bench/browser.js --lanes=5k`, packed tarball, 24 keystrokes per run, p95 of `totalMs`; the bench harness was broken at the reference — `applyUpdateStaged` is gone — fixed in the tests-first commit): reference 24.5 / 26.4 / 23.9 / 20.5 ms (median 24.2; an earlier loaded batch 28.5–33.2), after 23.5 / 23.7 / 25.8 / 21.4 ms (median 23.6); p50 21.4 → 21.1; `flushMs` p95 10.5–12.0 → 9.4–11.1. No regression beyond noise. **Lint (BI2-5)**: `no-restricted-syntax` over `src/lib` forbids DOM mutation calls (`appendChild` … `setAttribute`, `remove`) and `textContent`/`nodeValue`/`innerHTML`/`contentEditable` assignments outside the host writers: `components/`, the handles' attachments (`text`, `block`, `inlineBlock`), `surface/observer`, `surface/overlay`, `plugins/`, `collaboration/` (overlay chrome), and, until R7, `events/domTextMutationObserver.ts` and `events/onInput.ts`. **Deviations**: (1) Placement is through an anchor minted *at the edit* when its records are first named while the model still equals the render (the MutationObserver callback, `flushNow`, or `take()` before a transaction), else a three-way rebase within the block — not through run ids from `doc/index` (plan +5 not spent): run-start anchors cannot follow a split inside the run (the split-before row failed with them). (2) The render epoch is the surface's own (`surface.epoch`); the projector keeps `projector.render` and its dedupe unchanged (no behaviour change at R6). (3) Mark elements are registered but not strict containers: the rich-text mark snippets still render inner markup (`<b>`, `<a>`), since declared mark elements were left out of R5; the mark chain is checked on registered `data-edytor-mark` elements only. (4) Attributes are compared with today's table (`resolveManagedAttributeTarget` + the heal tried on a detached copy), not a new declared table; the table moves with R7. (5) Deadline expiry and attempt phase changes do not write the records signal yet; the drift expectation is read at the pass. (6) L39 (`refreshEditorDom`, `{#key editorDomRevision}`) stays: it needs the observer to re-insert missing elements, which is R7's action. (7) The snapshot is reported only for a content the flush re-renders (a cell patch or remount); a divergence the flush leaves in place is found by the post pass (the move row). (8) The browser census reads the last page load of each test only. **Design iterations**: none failed; on the way the comparator gained the settle check, the hand-back rule for the IME host (actions after the session ended restore the host), and today's observer's repairs re-dirty the content (they make no records). **Contract questions for R7**: (a) Run anchors or edit anchors? The plan's run ids from `doc/index` would need a split-aware rebase; the edit anchor needs the records before the model change (`take()` at `beforeTransaction`). (b) D-25 vs the two gate3 specs that expect a foreign node beside a text element to be removed (`input fallback` "removes unmanaged child nodes inserted outside managed text wrappers", "unmanaged SIBLING node at block level"): keep D-25 and rewrite them? (c) A foreign element with text inside a text element ("a spoofed mark inside a live text … is removed"): adopt its text (location default) or drop it? (d) After WebKit's converted-space normalization the extra text node would be adopted again by the location default (a doubled space): R7 must treat an unregistered text node of a strict text element as structure after its text was compared. (e) The vanishing code line (class 7) is a live defect on the reference: fix before R7, or let R7's re-insertion from records fix it? (f) Unify `surface.epoch` with `projector.render` at R7 (the display dedupes on (selection epoch, render epoch)). |

**compare pass**: attribute heal against the table (bounded: 100 heals, 250 ms window), identity clones removed with their added root, a node inside a content's run (between two of its registered elements) removed, the root's foreign children removed, then each named content against its cell: snapshot → adopt at its edit anchor's place; a missing/escaped element or structure only → restore (exact text when the partition is the render's — elements and the caret's node survive and the epoch bumps for the projector — else `cells.remount`); a native line break runs as its occurrence; a broken structure is re-rendered after); read-only divergence (text and structure) waits for the flip back and is then restored; the live IME host is skipped. (`tests/packed-consumer/run.sh`'s node smoke fails on `schemaVersion()` 4 ≠ 1 on both sides — a stale smoke expectation since D12, not R7; the tarball is packed before it.) **Deviations**: (1) **A content's run is strict**: a node between two registered text/atom elements of one content is removed (the run is the core's; D-25 covers the kind's markup around it) — Chrome's bogus `<br>` beside an inline atom (`inline-atomic.spec:1209`). (2) **Identity clone** = an unregistered element carrying `data-edytor-id|text|block|inline-block`, or `data-edytor-mark` naming a *declared* mark: removed without adoption (`dom-mutation` clone spec); an undeclared mark name claims nothing, so answer (c)'s spoof is foreign input. (3) **A record is inverted whole** (added nodes removed, every removed node back in order), not only its registered elements — the plan says registered elements and anchors; Prism needs the whole record. (4) **Model-owned window deferral**: divergence outside the drift host waits while any model-owned attempt is open (the mobile block-merge drift lands in the merge target, not the attempt's host); the plan lists drift on its host only. (5) **Mark attributes**: only the mark's owned attributes (name, void, `contenteditable`) diverging re-render its content; anything else on a mark stays (F-O13 (c)). (6) **The root-heal re-display** keeps one deferred display on the next task after healing the root (Chromium drops the range a task after the blur; `dom-mutation.spec:1276`, `adversarial-wave2` blur rows), gated on the last gesture — instead of the 106-line caret capture/restore. (7) **F-O10 gate** runs at the end of each test/schedule (dom: every view, discovered by the `facade` getter like the cells shadow; browser: `window.__EDYTOR__`; DST: every engine and collab peer), not after every settle inside a test; no jsdom writer log. (8) `domTextOffset.ts` stays (44): `getYIndex` maps offset 1 of an empty text to 0, which would lose F-I22's caret. (9) `surface.check()` is gone (the oracle is test-side). (10) The switch commit changes 3,081 production lines (+900 / −2,181), 1,892 of them the deletion of one file; no green split exists (two observers cannot both act). (11) Added after the tests-first commit, from the lanes: the F-I22 row, the `input.spec` D-25 rewrite, the DST D-25 oracle test. **Design iterations**: none failed; the fallback (+800) was not approached. **Contract questions**: (a) `CodePlugin` imports Prism with automatic highlighting: on any SSR-hydrated page Prism rewrites the code block's DOM once, which R7 now inverts — set `Prism.manual = true` in the plugin (a one-line fix, outside R7's scope)? (b) Deviation (1)'s content-run rule: accept it as part of D-25's wording (the run is core markup)? (c) Deviation (4): should unrelated divergence during a model-owned window be adopted immediately instead of waiting (today's behaviour waited)? (d) Browser-lane F-O10 covers the page's main view only: add a view registry hook if multi-view pages should be checked. |

## Orchestrator notes

- **G0 chromium**: 482 passed, 10 failed = the 9 known-red + `inline-atomic.spec.ts:1025` (deletes a native-selected
  inline mention without beforeinput). It passed 3/3 in isolation and its file passed 19/19: a flake under full-suite
  load, not a regression. Failures outside the known-red list are re-run in isolation before a verdict.
- **D1 chromium**: 483 passed, 9 failed = exactly the known-red list.
- **D1 contract answers** (orchestrator, from the plan):
  1. No legacy reader for the pre-D1 single `del` key. v14 documents exist only in development (plan §2.1). When the
     transport branch merges, the schema stamp is bumped so a pre-D1 document is refused cleanly by the generation
     gate instead of being misread with its deleted blocks visible.
  2. Deleting a non-live block returns `false` and `blockText` under a deleted parent is `null`: accepted as the
     one-liveness-answer rule; listed for the C1 release notes as an API change.

## C1 release notes (collected)

- D1: deleting a non-live block returns `false`; `blockText` under a deleted parent is `null` (one liveness answer).
- D12: schema generation 3 → 4 (streams; the `slices` attr is gone, merge claims live on `claims`); development
  documents are re-imported. Anchors are `{b, a}` — the `o` owner facet and `a: -2` are gone from the presence and
  history wire shape.
- D12: a forced re-migration (`force`) gives a block whose stream started at a boundary a new nonce without moving
  its attribution record, so its `createdBy` is replaced at the next attributed write (accepted).
- R5: `placeholder` is a string or a function of `{type, data, focused, empty}` (the snippet form is gone),
  rendered as `data-placeholder` + a shipped `::before` rule; snippets render inner markup (`element`,
  `viewState` on kinds; `use:block.attach` removed); pending marks are `selection.pending`/`stage()`
  (`Text.markOnNextInsert` removed); `Block.suggestions` is JSON; chrome lives in `[data-edytor-overlay]`.
- C1: D-15 API retirement (the advanced-internals exports, the per-block subscriber API, `decorateRuns`, IDB `get/set/del`, `rawSuggestions`); the server-coordinator surface is kept. Full changelog: README "Migrating from 0.0.11".
- P2.7 (phase 2): marks are declared by `tag` (+ `attributes(value)`); the core renders the tag as the mark element (`<strong data-edytor-mark="bold">`, was `<span data-edytor-mark="bold"><b>`) and the clipboard exports the same element; `MarkDefinition.html` is replaced by `tag`/`attributes`, `snippet` is optional. Link, color, highlight, superscript and subscript now export their element.
- P4.1 (phase 2): HTML import is back, in the core — external `text/html` paste and drop are parsed by `DOMParser` and mapped through the kind and mark records (`parse` hooks new on both); paste order internal MIME → embedded fragment → plugin `onPaste` → HTML → `text/plain`. HTML carrying nothing falls through to `text/plain`.
- K5 (phase 2, P3.2 — UPSTREAM.md P8): the raw engine `edytor/crdt` is pruned to what edytor runs on; 76 of its 161 exports are removed. Renderers: `AttributionsRenderer`, `createAttributionsRenderer`, `DiffRenderer`, `createDiffRenderer`, `SnapshotRenderer`, `createSnapshotRenderer` (`AbstractRenderer`/`$renderer` stay). Snapshots: `Snapshot`, `snapshot`, `createSnapshot`, `emptySnapshot`, `createDocFromSnapshot`, `encodeSnapshot`, `encodeSnapshotV2`, `decodeSnapshot`, `decodeSnapshotV2`, `equalSnapshots`, `snapshotContainsUpdate`, `nodeMapGetAllSnapshot` (and `node.getAttrs(snapshot)`'s argument). ## Phase 2 (plan: docs/architecture-v2/phase2-plan.md)

Decisions adopted (maintainer "ok implement the plan"): D1 prune the Yjs fork (yes); D2 BroadcastChannel leg stays retired; D3 keep a thin read-only public `selection.state`; D4 build the Durable Object room now. Rule: no feature deletion. Same counter (sha c15490d2…) and discipline as phase 1.

| P2.10 | 9af5d59, d1cab54 | 19,074 → 19,003 (9af5d59, −71); d1cab54 18,982 → 18,979 (−3, after P2.9): **−74** | 9af5d59: navigation/selection/hotkeys/inline-atomic/p1-selection/v5-classifier on chromium, firefox, webkit, mobile: 392 passed; `--repeat-each=3` 369/369. **Then the P2.9 full run lost a move in p1-selection:98 on chromium**; alone, `--repeat-each=15`: 13/15 red without the timers, 15/15 green with them. d1cab54: race row 20/20 (chromium), p1-selection 96/96 (`--repeat-each=8`, three engines) | **Census** `setTimeout(` 14 → 12, `requestAnimationFrame(` 2 → 1. The keyup listener, the `setTimeout 0` and rAF re-syncs and the 30 ms horizontal fallback after a native arrow are gone: the keydown marks the gesture, so V5's classifier adopts the arrow's `selectionchange` as intent. The timers were hiding a projector defect, fixed in its owner (d1cab54): BI-3 (mint an unobserved native move before a transaction this view did not issue) required the last pass to have rendered the current document version, so a commit that rendered nothing (the room's first sync) kept the rule off until the next render — the peer's first commit, whose pass then displayed the old caret over the arrow's move. BI-3 now asks whether the last pass answered every render and display request (`#done` = the `request:epoch` key); the `#rendered` version field is gone. No engine needs a kept rule. |

Nothing moved to a new module: there is no surface events or pointer owner today (surface/ holds the observer, projector, overlay, cells and pin), and moving the rules would not delete them. (2) `delete-shapes.spec.ts:319`: the owner was wrong on every engine, since the baseline: the collapsed line commands deleted to the block's edge for both units, so `deleteSoftLineBackward` over `one\ntwo` also erased `one\n`.
